## Document 016: 2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-09-30_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ_ef0b0461.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

中国が住宅販売モデルを転換へ 
～不動産不況は脱却の兆しなお見えず～

調査部 主任エコノミスト 月岡直樹  
080-1069-6684 naoki.tsukioka@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 中国政府が新規則を発表。住宅は予約販売から完成後販売にシフト 
中国政府は8月28日、住宅販売制度と住宅ローン管理に関する新規則を発表した。新規の住宅不動産
プロジェクトについて完成後販売の方式を促すほか、未完成物件の予約販売における住宅ローンの実
行時期を物件引き渡し後に変更する。住宅の予約販売制度が不動産ディベロッパーの過度なレバレッ
ジ追求による資金繰り悪化と、それに伴う住宅引き渡し遅延問題を引き起こしたことから、住宅販売
モデルの転換を加速させる。 
住宅都市農村建設部や中国人民銀行、国家金融監督管理総局などの関連省庁が一斉に通達や管理規
定を公布した。住宅販売制度に関する通達では、①予約販売の代金（購入者が支払う頭金と銀行から
振り込まれる住宅ローン）について住宅不動産プロジェクトごとの専用口座を通じた銀行側による資
金管理を強化すること、②新たに不動産用地や建設許可を取得するプロジェクトに対して「完成物件
での販売を優先的に選択」させ、建設許可を取得済みの新規プロジェクトについても完成物件での販
売を奨励すること、③住宅不動産プロジェクトごとに会社を設立し、メインバンクを選定して開発資
金の融資を受けること、などの方針を示した。一方、住宅ローンの新たな管理規定には、予約販売に
おける住宅ローンについて「厳格にプロジェクト竣工届出後に実行しなければならない」と明記した
ほか、これまで最長30年とされてきた住宅ローンの借入期間を40年に変更することを盛り込んだ。 
■ 「保交房」に一定のメド。ディベロッパーの法的整理が進む可能性も 
中国の不動産市場はこれまで、ディベロッパーが物件の竣工を待たずに販売を開始する予約販売方
式が一般的であり、住宅ローンも購入者が予約購入した時点で実行され、返済義務が生じる慣行とな
っていた。予約販売には、購入者にとって不動産価格の値上がり前に物件を確保できるメリットが、
ディベロッパーにとって不動産開発に投じた資金を早期に回収できるメリットがあった。 
しかし、財務レバレッジの追求による収益の最大化を狙うディベロッパーは、回収した予約販売代
金を新たな不動産開発用地の獲得や不動産とは関係のないビジネスへの投資に流用し、事業を急拡大
させた。その実態は自転車操業であり、市場の過熱を懸念した中国政府が2021年にディベロッパーへ
の資金調達規制 （いわゆる 「3つのレッドライン」 ） を正式導入するとたちまち資金繰りに行き詰まり、
社債の債務不履行（デフォルト）が頻発する。ディベロッパーの信用力が低下したところにゼロコロ
2026 年 9 月 30 日

### Page 2

2

ナ政策の強化が追い打ちをかける形で販売が急減し、ディベロッパーの資金繰りが一段と悪化したこ
とで建設工事の進捗が滞り、2022年に入って予約販売済み物件の引き渡し遅延が深刻化する。 
ここに至って予約販売のデメリットが明らかとなる。すなわち、物件引き渡しリスクを負担するの
は購入者であり、たとえ遅延しても住宅ローンの返済を続けなければならないことである。事態を重
く見た中国政府は、「保交楼（予約販売済み未完成物件の引き渡し保証。「保交房」も同じ）」のス
ローガンを掲げ、ディベロッパーの資金繰り支援に乗り出す。2024年1月には、当局が融資適格と判断
した不動産開発プロジェクトをまとめたホワイトリストを作成し、各銀行がこのリストに基づいてデ
ィベロッパーに資金を融通するスキームを構築して、問題の解消を急がせたのである。 
中国政府がこのタイミングで新規則を発表した背景には、この「保交房」に一定のメドが付いたと
判断したことがあるとみられる。事実、国務院の李強首相は2026年3月の全人代における政府活動報告
で「『保交房』の任務は全面的に完成した」と宣言している。また、見逃せない動きとして、広東省
広州市の中級人民法院（地方裁判所）が8月21日、実質的に経営破綻している某ディベロッパーについ
て、債権者による清算の申し立てを受理したことが挙げられる。 
デフォルトを起こした不動産ディベロッパーの多くはこれまで、倒産によって債務の返済や予約販
売物件の引き渡し、建設業者への代金支払いなどの義務・責任を放棄することが許されず、債権者か
ら債務の減免や返済猶予の合意を得つつゾンビ化した状態で経営を続けてきた。ディベロッパーの破
綻が相次げば、不良債権問題の深刻化によって金融システムが動揺するリスクを招くだけでなく、予
約販売物件の引き渡しが困難となって社会問題に発展したり、下請け業者の連鎖倒産で景気が急激に
冷え込む可能性もあったからである。 
今回の動きは、清算の申し立てを裁判所が「受理」しただけではあり、実際に清算が進むのかはな
お予断を許さないが、中国政府がディベロッパーの法的整理を前に進める意思を示したとみることも
できる。今後、清算手続きが順調に進むのか、他のディベロッパーについても同様の動きが広がるの
かを注意深く見守る必要があろう。 
■ 住宅販売モデル転換の社会的意義は大きいが、不動産市況への影響は軽微 
では、住宅販売モデルの転換は不動産市場にどのような影響を与えるであろうか。その前にまず、
不動産市場の現状を整理しておきたい。 
資金調達規制の強化をきっかけとした不動産不況は6年目に突入した。中国の不動産市場規模（販売
金額）は2021～25年にかけて半分に縮小し、販売面積や開発投資額は4割、新規着工面積に至っては7
割も減少した（図表1）。2026年も不況脱却の兆しは見えず、1～8月の販売面積は前年比▲12.1％、開
発投資額は同▲19.9％と低迷が続いている。開発投資の先行指標とされる不動産開発向け銀行貸出も
同▲33.3％となっており、ディベロッパーにとって資金繰りが厳しい状況は変わっていない。 
販売が大きく落ち込んだことで、市場には過剰な在庫が積み上がっている。みずほ総合研究所調査
部の試算によれば、建設中の物件を含む中国の住宅在庫（商用およびオフィス用を除く）は2025年末
時点で40.8億平米に達する（図表2）。2025年の住宅販売面積が7.3億平米であったことから、新規着工
がゼロになると仮定しても在庫をすべて消化するのに5年以上かかる計算である。中国政府は2024年5
月、地方政府による住宅在庫の買い取り策を発表し、同年10月には地方政府が専項債（特別債）の発
行で得た資金を買い取りに使うことも容認したが、在庫の消化は遅々として進んでいない。

### Page 3

3

住宅価格（主要70都市平均）は2021年7月のピーク時から新築で▲14％、中古で▲23％下落し、全体
として下落傾向に歯止めがかかっていない。ただ、図表3のとおり、底堅い需要のある大都市（一級都
市：北京、上海、広州、深圳）と膨大な在庫を抱える地方都市（二級都市：各省の省都。三級都市：
省都以下の主要都市）で二極化が顕著になっている。特に上海は堅調で、その新築住宅価格は2021年
7月から＋24％も上昇している。とはいえ、その堅調さは資産としての不動産保有の魅力を有する一部
の都市に限ったものであり、市況の回復が地方都市にまで広く波及する様子はうかがえない。 
住宅引き渡し遅延問題の発生以降、引き渡しリスクのない完成物件の購入が増加しつつあるが、予
約販売は2025年時点でも依然として住宅販売面積全体の6割超を占めている（図表4）。住宅販売モデ
ルの転換、すなわち予約販売から完成後販売への移行と予約販売における住宅ローン実行の後ろ倒し
は、住宅購入者の物件引き渡しリスクを大幅に軽減することになり、消費者保護の観点からもその社
会的意義は非常に大きいといえる。しかし、これに伴ってディベロッパーの資金繰りはより厳しいも
のとなる。ディベロッパーの資金調達に占める販売代金（頭金などの前受金と住宅ローン）の比率は
4割を超えているからである（図表5）。このため、短期的には新規の開発（土地の取得や新規着工）

が減少する可能性が高い。ただ、新規則は完成
後販売を全国一律に「強制」するのではなく、
あくまで「優先的に選択」させるとしているこ
とから、地方によってはディベロッパーの資金
繰りにも配慮した柔軟な対応がとられる可能
性もある。 
新規制が不動産市況に与える影響は、住宅ロ
ーンの最長借入期間の延長が新たな需要を掘
り起こすとしても、軽微であろう。不動産不況
を脱するためには、過剰在庫の消化とディベロ
ッパーの法的整理、不良債権の処理を着実に進
め、市場を健全な状態に戻すことが不可欠とい
えるが、それにはまだ相当の時間を要するもの
とみられる。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (115)金管投顧新字第 016 號          Page 2 of 7 
個股報告 
長榮 為 全 球 貨 櫃 運 力 第七大 航
商，市占率 5.9% 
 長榮成立於 1968 年 9 月，實收資本額 216.5 億元。截至 3Q26，全球超過 1.3
萬位員工及 293 個代理行，航線擴及 101 個國家，總船隊數共 242 艘(自有船
比例超過 6 成)，預計至 2030 年仍有 72 艘船待交付。根據 Alphaliner 統計，
截至目前長榮為全球貨櫃運力第七大航商，市佔率約 5.9%。長榮 1H26 營收
結構為貨運收入 93%，船艙租收入 1.2%，其他 5.8%。

歐 美 航 線 運 價 優 於 預 期 ， 預 估
3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元 
 長榮 Sep-26 營收 497.11 億元(+1.48%MoM，+63.75%Y oY)，3Q26 營收達成
率 105.5%，優於預期，主要受惠歐美航線運價走升，且旺季附加費及燃油附
加費挹注營收 。獲利方面 ，受惠運價上漲帶動獲利率提升 ， 惟中東衝突於Jul-26
一度趨緩後再度升溫，且考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對
成本形成壓力，預估 3Q26 毛利率 38.91%。由於營收規模擴大，費用率有望
下降 ，預估營益率 35.71%，假設 匯率 持穩 ，預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元
(+164.9%QoQ，+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

歐美航線需求放緩 ，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 14.89 元 
 根據上海航交所 10/9 公告，上海出口集裝箱運價指數 (SCFI) 3,664.11 點
(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估
稅後 EPS 45.75 元 
 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

### Page 2

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

### Page 1

Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.

3Q26 earnings preview: steeper 2nm ramp 
lifting sales upside at resilient margins

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 3,260 TWD | Price: 2,550 TWD

Solid HPC and flagship mobile pipeline solidifies 2H26 
TSMC’s 3Q26 sales were above street +18% QoQ in NT$ and reached US$46.7bn (vs. its 
guide of US$44.6-45.8bn) which we believe should be driven by the builds for Apple’s 
mobile SoC and AMD’s server CPU on 2nm along with multiple GPU and ASIC product 
refreshes migrating to 3nm. The mix upgrades could lift 4Q26 sales by +10-15% QoQ, 
putting it on track to the guidance for 40%+ YoY in ’26. While utilization and pricing are 
favorable, GMs could hold steady in 3Q/4Q26 (BofAe 67.0%/67.8%) rather than expand 
(recent street 67-68%/68-69%) factoring dilution from 2nm and overseas fab ramps. 
Tight supply enables greater value capture and investment 
On solid AI demand outlook, TSMC’s capex should be on track to its lifted guidance of 
US$60-64bn in ’26 (BofAe US$63bn). The investment could see another surge in ’27 
(BofAe US$83bn) for further N3, N2, A16 and CoWoS-L expansion and new technologies 
ramp (e.g. CoPoS and CPO). The tool makers’ modest price hike could require TSMC to 
spend 10% more capex though should be reflected in its value when the capacity starts 
production in ’28 on top of a 5-10% leading edge ASP lift in 1Q27 on supply tightness. 
Checkpoints: AI demand, capex & competition/partnership 
1) TSMC’s view on cloud AI investment amid rate hike + challenging FCF, 2) comment on 
the market and its growth and capex commitment, 3) update on the competition and 
collaboration (see Exhibit 29 for our analysis on Terafab/SpaceX) and US investment.  
AI-led earnings outlook firmer; valuation should catch up 
We lift our 26/27/28 EPS by 2-9% to NT$112/NT$161/NT$203 and keep our Buy rating 
on TSMC and raise PO to NT$3,260/US$620 on 20x ‘27E P/E. Its current valuation is 
undemanding at 16x ‘27E P/E (vs. its long term 11-21x) backed by its advanced 
technology leadership.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

3Q26 sales topped guidance; GMs tracking
well but recent street could be too bullish
We profile our expectation for TSMC’s 3Q26 earnings on October 15th. 3Q26 sales were
reported +18% QoQ in NT$, above BofAe/street expectations of +14%/+13% QoQ.
Based on the average FX of NT$31.99/US$ (vs. its NT$32US$ guide), its sales in US$
term would be US$46.7bn, also above its guidance range of US$44.6-45.8bn. we believe
the strength is led by strong ramp on 2nm potentially growing to 11% of its sales in

3Q26 (vs. 3% in 2Q26) for Apple’s builds for its product launches and surging demand
for AMD’s Venice server CPU. These growth drivers on the node are supplemented by
the incremental inventory stocking from Mediatek and Qualcomm for their flagship
smartphone SoCs albeit at a low base and below seasonal due to weak Android demand.
On top of the 2nm scale up, we expect the utilization for 5nm and 3nm to stay above
100% backed by the continued HPC/AI build strength especially with multiple product

upgrades including 1) accelerators (GPUs and ASICs): strong demand for Nvidia’s Rubin
GPU, AMD’s MI400, AWS’s Trainium3 ramp and initial ramp for Google’s TPUv8t/i more
than offsetting a mild wind down in Google’s Ironwood TPU, 2) CPUs: solid demand for
general purpose servers and AI data centers as noted in our deep dive report quantifying
CPU demand through ’30 across x86 (AMD’s Turin CPUs) and ARM based CPUs (e.g.
Nvidia’s Vera CPU, Google’s Axion CPU, AWS Graviton CPU along with incremental

contribution from Microsoft Cobalt CPU) and 3) networking switch: more and faster
data transmission in the complex AI data centers requires more advanced  networking
switch solutions from Nvidia/Mellanox, Broadcom and Marvell.
The company’s better than expected sales in 3Q26 backed by healthy shipment and
pricing trend on the advanced nodes should be supportive to its GMs. However, in
contrast to the recent aggressive street expectations of 67-68% GMs in 3Q26, we only

model it at the high-end of its guidance of 65-67%, balancing more favorable utilization
and gradual 3nm depreciation roll off partially offset by a 3-4% GMs dilution from 2nm
ramp which we believe is driver for the incremental sales during the quarter.

### Page 5

HPC/Apple lifting 4Q26 sales outlook;
resilient GM expectation but below street
For 4Q26, based on its slightly above +40% YoY full year sales guide, TSMC’s sales could
grow 10-15% QoQ (BofAe +11%), better than its 10-year seasonality of 7% QoQ even
off a high base from 1Q-3Q26. The blended ASPs strength (BofAe +11% QoQ) should be
the key driver for 4Q26 sales as shipment mix continues to shift toward advanced nodes,
with revenue from 5nm and below processes representing 75% of the company’s sales

(vs. 66%/71% in 2Q/3Q26). Specifically, 2nm with 15%+ higher wafer pricing than 3nm
could further grow its contribution to 18% of TSMC’s sales during the quarter. On the
other hand, we model a stable wafer shipment (BofAe +9%/-5% QoQ for
advanced/mature nodes), balancing HPC demand strength and Apple’s seasonal chipset
builds for its new product launches and a modest drop on the specialty process output
and effective capacity loss due to equipment movement for the node conversion.

While the blended pricing is even more favorable and utilization on the advanced and
mature nodes in general stay healthy, we model a relatively stable GMs only up to
~67.8% levels in 4Q26 (vs. recent street and investors’ expectations at 68-69%). The key
factors we consider as mildly negative offset would be 1) higher depreciation: +25-30%
HoH in 2H26 due to more overseas and 2nm capacity ramp and 2) 2nm still going
through yield learning curve and could see more meaningful impact in 4Q26 (vs. its 3-4%

GMs dilution guide in 2H26) as the sales contribution grows.

### Page 6

2026-27 sales and margins outlook 
solidified by healthy AI demand 
Factoring in stronger CPU and AI GPU/ASIC demand outlook as noted in our Semicon 
Taiwan takeaways report where we updated our industry models, we are lifting our US$ 
sales expectation for TSMC in ‘26/’27 to +42%/+40% YoY respectively (vs. BofA prior 
+41%/+40% YoY). More importantly, with utilization staying tighter for longer along with 
inflationary cost pass through (e.g. equipment and consumables), TSMC should raise its 
advanced node and packaging pricing by 5-10% in the beginning of 2027 to reflect its 
value. This along with a mild 3-5% price hike on the mature nodes where the utilization 
is back to 90-95% as it optimizes the product mix following the strategic 6”/8” capacity 
retirement should lift its GMs to 67%/69% in 2026/27 (vs. BofA prior 67%/67%).

Capex on track for lifted guide for 2026 
and will grow 20%+ CAGR from ’26-28 
We lifted our front-end foundry industry capex growth expectation to 19%/26% YoY for 
‘26/27 to US$86bn/US$108bn, supporting a CAGR of 16% from ’26-28. This is mostly 
led by our higher expectation on TSMC’s capex on stronger wafer demand on N3 and N2 
from GPUs, ASICs, CPUs, networking switches and HBM base dies and initial ramp of 
A16, along with incremental spending reacceleration from tier 2 foundries. 
Separately, we also raised the back-end packaging and testing industry spending 
estimates from 66%/36% YoY to 82%/21% YoY for ‘26/27 to US$27bn/US$33bn, 
supporting a CAGR of 15% from ’26-28. The upward revision is driven by our higher 
expectation of TSMC’s more aggressive capacity addition on CoWoS-R/L to 
accommodate more demand from its customers amid growing level of chipset 
complexity and reticle size and higher level of capacity readiness of SoIC for higher 3D 
packaging adoption for logic and memory dies integration and CPO. The more 
aggressive spending from ASE is set to grow by another 30-50% YoY in 2027 off a high 
base in 2026 on its own FoCoS capacity ramp and TSMC’s CoWoS licensing.

Specifically on TSMC, based on our bottom up capacity analysis across front-end 
manufacturing and back-end packaging, TSMC’s capex spending is tracking to US$63bn 
for 2026 (vs. its latest guidance of US$60-64bn) mostly skewed toward advanced front-
end manufacturing including 55-60k WPM 3nm capacity addition, 60k WPM 2nm 
capacity ramp, initial 3-5k WPM A16 capacity installation. The back-end packaging 
investment should be largely driven by its CoWoS capacity growth while the spending on 
the equipment pull-in for CPO and CoPoS remains limited as most of the equipment are 
still under sampling process for qualification and meaningful shipment will be in 2027-
28. 
In addition to the earlier tool pull-in schedule due to tighter equipment supply with 
memory makers expanding, the fast-growing capex should reflect the rising cost per 1k 
WPM on the advanced nodes vs. mature nodes, with the equipment cost per 1k WPM 
advanced wafer capacity expansion has grown from US$156mn in 2023 to US$241mn in 
2025. Even factoring in more common use of equipment on the advanced nodes, the 
capex/1k WPM advanced capacity could further rise by 20% to US$289mn in 2027 with 
the technology architecture upgrade. 
On top of the investment in the core front-end manufacturing, we expect the company 
to slightly outspend in the back-end packaging and estimate it is at the upper half of its 
guidance for 10-20% total capex, mainly for more aggressive capacity expansion to 
meet its customers’ demand amid growing competition from Intel’s EMIB-T solution and 
technology innovation. On the other hand, the maintenance capex could be reduced for 
specialty technologies as the company gradually retires the equipment.

With TSMC’s stronger bargaining power against the equipment supply chain vs. its 
peers, it should be able to fully pass on the ~10% higher nominal capex to its customers 
by lifting its foundry pricing when the tools are delivered for its production ramp in 
2H27, setting up a good start for the pricing outlook in 2028.

depreciation, we estimate it will only grow at 21% CAGR from 2026-28 due to modestly 
higher portion of the spending towards facility and clean room with much longer 
depreciation cycle than equipment. This should keep the depreciation still undergrowing 
its sales of +32% CAGR, sustaining GMs expansion to 68-69% levels by 2028.

### Page 10

Capacity expansion accelerates across 
advanced front-end and back-end 
As TSMC is in the process to retire a portion of its equipment on 6”, 8” and mature 12” 
processes while still adding the capacity on the advanced nodes, we estimate the 
company’s total front-end manufacturing capacity will drop by 2% YoY in 2026. 
Exhibit 17: TSMC's year-end capacity by node for 20nm and below 
TSMC will expand aggressively on 3nm and 2nm through 2027

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

Exhibit 18: TSMC's advanced node capacity as % of its installed base 
TSMC's advanced nodes will reach 40% of its total capacity

Source: Company Data, BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

The trend could be more stable into 2027-28 as TSMC strives to optimize its capacity 
mix and across node conversion to accommodate its customers’ demand especially on 
the leading-edge technologies, with its advanced capacity set to grow by 10% CAGR 
from 2025-28 balanced by the mature capacity reduction at -8% CAGR. 
Based on our estimate, TSMC’s capacity for 7nm and below nodes will grow from 320k 
WPM per month in 2023 to ~700k WPM by 2028 at a 16% CAGR, through expansion and 
efficient conversion from higher equipment commonality, cross technology planning and 
AI productivity gains (e.g. tool idle time and chamber cleaning schedule optimization). 
This will also support the mix of 7nm and below capacity climb toward 40-45% of its 
total capacity (vs. 24% in 2023). By region, we expect the capacity expansion through 
2027 to enable its overseas fabs to account for 17% of TSMC’s total capacity by 2028 
(vs. 7% in 2020) and similarly for the advanced node capacity (vs. ~0% in 2023), mainly 
in Arizona and Kumamoto. 
Specifically, we believe the company is on track to its target on 25% CAGR capacity 
expansion for its 5nm and 3nm from ’22-27. We model its 3nm capacity will reach 
190k/230k WPM by 4Q26/27. On N2 family, TSMC plans to grow capacity by 70% CAGR 
from ’26-28, with 1st year ramp delivering 45% more capacity vs. 3nm as it rolls out 5 
fabs at once.

We would note 3nm, as a sweet spot for high performance computing, networking and

On the back-end packaging, with surging server CPU demand on top of the already 
strong order backlog for GPUs and ASICs, we estimate the industry’s CoWoS supply will 
need to grow by 52% CAGR from 2026-28. TSMC will still lead the capacity expansion 
from 120k WPM in 4Q26 to 210k WPM in 4Q27, while the OSATs will also grow their 
portion of the supply with ASE and Amkor adding capacity to 70-75k+ WPM (vs. 30-35k 
in 4Q26) and 25-30k WPM (vs. 15-20k by 4Q26) in 4Q27 respectively. Specifically, while 
TSMC should still lead the supply for complex GPU and ASIC packaging, ASE grows as a 
major 2nd source with its capacity share growing from 18% of the industry in ’26 to 29% 
in ’28 and proven track record as the major CoWoS full process service provider for 
AMD’s Venice server CPU.

### Page 14

Solid technology roadmap and execution 
easing competition concern 
TSMC is pursuing a dual track strategy for its front-end manufacturing, advancing the 
solution for both its consumer and HPC customers. The nodes targeted for consumer 
chipsets requiring cost efficiency and IP reuse will be introduced on an annual basis to 
match its customers’ yearly product launch schedule. For the HPC applications, the 
company targets to roll out a new node with biennial cadence, supporting major leaps in 
performance, transistor density, and power density. 
Exhibit 27: Front-end manufacturing roadmap comparison 
TSMC's advanced node technology roadmap advancing faster vs. Intel and Samsung and has the widest offerings targeted for different applications

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
This is evidenced in its N2 family and below nodes, with N2U ramping in ’28 for mobile 
applications and A13 node in ’29 which will be backward compatible to A14. It will also 
have A12 node in ’29 integrated SPR to address power integrity and current delivery 
constraint suitable for AI/HPC systems. The strategy along with the continued expansion 
of the derivative offerings for main nodes should position TSMC well to defend its 
market share with its competitors claiming their progress on the advanced 
manufacturing. 
Specifically, Intel’s 18A has been in mass production since 4Q25 for its Panther Lake 
and Clearwater Forest chipsets though yield will not be fully fine tuned until 1Q27 at 25-
30k WPM capacity scale in its Oregon and Arizona fabs. The follow on derivative 
technology, 18A-P, has recently started risk production featuring 9% performance gains 
though may not enter into mass production until 2027 at the earliest. 
Longer term, Intel’s next major upgrade will be on 14A which is guided to start risk 
production in 2028 for commercialization in 2029. It is important to note A14 will be the 
first major node Intel provides with its external foundry customers and 0.9 PDK is 
targeted to be shipped in 4Q26. 
For Samsung, at its recent SAFE Forum in 2Q26, the company reaffirmed four 
generations of 2nm roadmap, including SF2 which has been in production since 2025, 
SF2P in 2026, SF2P+ in 2027-28 and SF2X (no firm date). Notably, the previously 
announced derivative technologies, including SF2Z featuring backside power for HPC 
applications and SF2A for automotive applications, were not included in its latest 
roadmap which should be folded as part of its SF2P+ offerings. With a modest delay for 
its 2nm progress, the company also pushed out its SF1.4 mass production to 2029 (vs. 
original 2027). 
On the capacity and yield planning for its advanced nodes, Samsung noted its SF2 yield 
is still at 50-60% levels but confirmed its SF2P+ tooling has been in installation through 
2026 for mass production in 2027 in its Taylor fab. Its capacity expansion in the US 
along with the existing 20k WPM capacity on SF2 in Korea put the company’s capacity 
installed base on the node at 35k WPM by the end of 2027.

### Page 15

Exhibit 28: TSMC's front-end manufacturing transistor density vs. Intel 
TSMC is widening its transistor density advantage against Intel as technology advances

Source: BofA Global Research estimates 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Based on the capacity planning and technology roadmap, we believe TSMC retains the 
leadership through its roadmap and yield execution and scale. Specifically, the 
company’s transistor density has been outperforming by ~30% for its N3/N3E vs. Intel 
3/18A. With similar production timing and targeted applications, we estimate the 
transistor density gap could be even widened to ~50%/~60% for TSMC’s upcoming 
A16/A12 nodes when comparing with Intel 18A-P/14A. 
In addition to delivering superior specs on the power, performance and area, TSMC’s 
execution reliability has become self-reinforcing: superior yield and on-time delivery on 
the leading edge technologies win customer trust. The profitable business opportunity 
then translates the cash flow funds to the next leg of capex cycle required for further 
technology and capacity lead. In contrast to TSMC’s 150-200k+ WPM capacity scale on 
every major node at mature yield, Samsung and Intel running their business at 15-20% 
of the company’s scale at 60-70% yield is not sufficient to meet their own demand nor 
the stringent requirement from the external customers especially as the opportunity cost 
gets higher on the advanced manufacturing. 
On the back-end packaging and testing solutions, TSMC provides its customers with 
higher complexity of the system level integration across advanced logic dies, CPO 
components and HBM through its CoWoS and SoIC technologies for the compute 
density and efficiency optimization. To accommodate larger size package, it is rolling out 
5.5-reticle CoWoS in ’26 with yield reaching 98%+ and will push the technology to 
9.5x/14x reticle limit in ’27/’28 
Longer term, the company is developing SoW for up to 40-reticle packaging and CoPoS 
potentially for late ‘28 / early ’29 mass deployment. The company is also upgrading CPO 
with COUPE solutions from PCB and substrate to interposer for 10x power efficiency 
uplift and 20x latency reduction though the contribution would still be limited based on 
our estimate at least through ’27 before accelerating to 1% of its sales in ’28 with more 
scale up switch adoption. 
While CoPoS mass ramp is toward ’29, the CoWoS upgrade at good yield should keep its 
position vs. Intel’s EMIB-T currently at 80-85% pilot yield seeing risk of losing business 
if it fails to deliver 95%+ mass production yield by mid-’27.

### Page 16

SpaceX could be an anchor customer for
TSMC, but not co-owning capacity/tech
On top of the competition risk from the incumbent players, the headline of Elon Musk’s
aggressiveness to build Terafab targeting long term 1 terawatt equivalent chipset
capacity for AI compute deployment in the space term also triggers the investor
concerns on TSMC’s positioning. We believe the risk is overstated as there could be a
delay in the qualification process of Intel’s 18A for external customers and 14A due to IP

and manufacturing flow issues based on the recent supply chain feedback. In addition,
the equipment vendors also noted Terafab is only evaluating on equipment spec and
potential procurement rather than placing firm orders. With extending equipment
delivery lead time, this could put the company in an unfavorable position to receive tools
for the initial production ramp in ’29 it scheduled.
In the mid-term, Terafab’s applications under Texas’s JETI program earlier this year

outlined ~US$119bn of planned investment across 4 phases from ’26-36, with ~60% of
it on tool procurement. The spending in ’26-28 would be highly skewed toward facility
construction before it starts to pull in US$6-8bn equipment per annum from ‘29-36.
We estimate the company will need to spend US$600mn+ for every 1k WPM expansion
on the advanced nodes it targets in the US. Factoring the investment it targets to spend
in the next decade, Terafab could only grow its capacity installed base by ~10k WPM per

annum at maximum to 96k WPM front-end manufacturing capacity installed by ’36, well
below TSMC’s scale, even if it can successfully deliver the technology roadmap through
its own R&D effort and Intel partnership. This would prevent Terafab’s operation from
the economies of scale as the node migration continues, keeping TSMC as the major
foundry partner for Tesla/SpaceX.
Exhibit 29: Wafer supply/demand analysis on Elon Musk's AI deployment target
With Terafab's near term delay and limited long term scale, TSMC will continue to be critical for Musk's ambitious AI compute deployment plan

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH
We also analyze the wafer consumption from SpaceX’s annual orbital AI deployment
target in ‘28/’29 of 10GW/100GW and Musk’s long term 1TW target. Assuming the

compute it plans to deploy in ’28-29 is all powered by the most advanced Nvidia’s
Feynman GPUs delivering 6kW per package, this would drive 1.7mn/16.7mn chipset
demand in ’28/’29 respectively which requires 10k/101k monthly advanced wafer
production. From the supply perspective, as Feynman GPU will be built on TSMC’s A16
node, the chipset demand should be fully accommodated. With limited support from
Terafab, SpaceX in this scenario could become one of the largest customers for TSMC

consuming ~20% of its advanced node capacity in ‘29 (vs. ~1% in ’26 and ~2% in ’28).
Longer term, Musk’s ambitious target to deploy 1 terawatt AI compute power annually
would consume 1mn+ wafers per month on the advanced nodes. To have higher level of
the control on capacity sufficiency, Terafab could consider to partner with TSMC though
the likelihood of TSMC licensing technology or operating a fab with Terafab holding a
meaningful share should be low. Specifically, we would note TSMC’s JV fab record is

limited only with WaferTech, SSMC, VIS, JASM, ESMC and AVSM mostly on mature
nodes and it did not co-own a fab with Apple who contributed ~21% of its revenue from
’15-25 mostly on the advanced processes as it keeps full business control and preserves
customer neutrality.

TSMC’s current valuation remains well supported when viewed through both its

### Page 18

Specifically, the valuation deviation for TSMC’s HPC customers vs. the company has 
widened with 100-150% valuation premium vs. TSMC at this stage (vs. historical 
average of 53%). We believe this is driven by a surge in valuation for the direct 
beneficiaries from AI data center deployment, including the companies designing GPUs, 
ASICs and networking chipsets. 
Exhibit 34: TSMC's consensus earnings vs. P/E trend 
TSMC's share price strength only driven by earnings upgrade so far

Source: BofA Global Research, Bloomberg 
BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
It is also noteworthy that, while TSMC’s share performance since 2H23 has been solid, it 
is predominantly driven by the earnings upward revisions rather than multiple expansion. 
The company’s NTM P/E has remained largely stable, only lifted to 18x in the past 2 
years vs. its long-term ~17x levels. The company’s ADR though has been performing 
better as it attracts a broader investor base vs. common share, with its premium still at 
10% (vs. the long term average 8%) and at 20x 2027 consensus P/E. 
With its firmer technology leadership, this should bode well for the company’s earnings 
upgrade cycle and its share should also be re-rated at least 20x NTM P/E (24x P/E for 
ADR factoring the premium), back closer to its customers and the broader ecosystem.

### Page 22

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of

6426 統新

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

可望取得一定市場地位。

## Document 202: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100 조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26 년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4 분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

## Document 205: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 tăng trưởng chậm 
nhưng biên lợi nhuận gộp vẫn được 
duy trì do tăng giá bán

Sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 chững lại 
do tâm lý nhập hàng thận trọng và 
hoạt động xây dựng phục hồi chậm

Biên lợi nhuận gộp được dự báo sẽ 
thu hẹp cuối năm, phản ánh chi phí 
nguyên liệu cao hơn trong 1Q2026

Khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP, giá mục 
tiêu 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Doanh thu/LNST 2Q2026 đạt lần lượt 1,320/367 tỷ VND (+1%/+11% YoY). Lũy kế 
6M2026, doanh thu đạt 2,777 tỷ VND (+3% YoY) trong khi LNST ước đạt 671 tỷ VND 
(+9% YoY). Biên lãi gộp 6M2026 cải thiện đạt 47.0% (+240 bps YoY), chủ yếu do việc 
tăng giá bán giúp doanh nghiệp bù đắp được mức tăng của chi phí đầu vào.

Lũy kế 6M2026, sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck. Việc 
BMP thu hẹp các chương trình chiết khấu, với mức chiết khấu bình quân khoảng 
5.2% trong 2Q2026, làm giảm động lực nhập hàng của các đại lý và khiến sản lượng 
tiêu thụ chậm lại. Dù vậy, thị phần toàn quốc của BMP ước đạt 28% (+1 đpt svck) 
nhờ các doanh nghiệp khác trong ngành gặp khó khăn khi giá hạt nhựa tăng mạnh.

Biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP dự kiến thu hẹp trong 2H2026 trước áp lực chi phí 
nguyên liệu đầu vào tăng. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi kỳ vọng mức độ ảnh hưởng sẽ thấp 
hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành do (1) các lô nguyên liệu giá cao từ 
1Q2026 đã được đưa vào sản xuất và tiêu thụ một phần trong 1H2026; (2) BMP chủ 
động nâng gấp đôi hàng tồn kho để ứng phó với biến động bất lợi của giá nguyên 
liệu; (3) hơn 90% nhu cầu nguyên liệu uPVC được cung ứng từ các đối tác trong 
nước như hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam, AGC Chemicals và LSP; và (4) công ty duy 
trì giá bán bình quân ở mức cao.

Dựa trên kết quả kinh doanh và định giá, chúng tôi khuyến nghị TRUNG LẬP với cổ 
phiếu BMP, giá mục tiêu là 166,100 VND/cổ phiếu (tương ứng với mức tăng 14.3% 
so với giá đóng cửa ngày 23/09/2026).

Tăng trưởng doanh thu và lợi nhuận thuần 5 năm gần nhất Hoạt động kinh doanh

Nhựa Bình Minh (BMP) là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực sản xuất ống nhựa với thị phần 
dẫn đầu tại thị trường nội địa (27% vào cuối 
2023), thị trường chính tập trung tại khu vực 
miền Nam. BMP có 4 nhà máy với tổng công suất 
đạt 150,000 tấn/năm. 
Nguồn: BMP, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Kỳ vọng đà phục hồi của xây dựng dân dụng và bất động sản miền Nam sẽ thúc đẩy sản lượng tiêu 
thụ tăng trưởng trong 2026–2027. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Giá PVC được kỳ vọng sẽ hạ nhiệt và khả năng giữ giá bán đầu ra ở mức cao giúp BMP giữ biên lợi 
nhuận gộp ở nền cao. 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

### Page 4

4

Nhu cầu nhập hàng thận trọng và 
cắt giảm các đợt chiết khấu trong 
kỳ của BMP làm giảm sản lượng tiêu 
thụ trong 2Q2026

Sự hồi phục của nhu cầu xây dựng 
dân dụng và nguồn cung các dự án 
bất động sản tại miền Nam sẽ thúc 
đẩy tăng trưởng sản lượng 2H2026 
 Sản lượng tiêu thụ trong 2Q2026 ước đạt 20,100 tấn, giảm 11% svck, nâng tổng 
sản lượng tiêu thụ 6M2026 ước đạt 43,920 tấn, giảm 5% svck do các yếu tố bất 
lợi từ bên ngoài như (1) giá hạt nhựa tăng cao khiến thị trường dè chừng trong 
việc nhập hàng vì lo ngại nguồn cung bất ổn (2) BMP đã thu hẹp các chương 
trình chiết khấu so với 2025 ngay trước thời điểm xung đột bùng nổ, giúp tỷ lệ 
chi phí bán hàng/doanh thu về mức 11.3%, giảm 800 bps so với 1Q2026 ở mức 
19.3%, nhưng đồng thời cũng khiến sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP suy giảm do 
chính sách bán hàng thắt chặt hơn với các đối thủ có thị phần thấp hơn.

Chúng tôi kỳ vọng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP dần hồi phục từ 3Q2026 khi nhu 
cầu nhập hàng của các đại lý cải thiện sau giai đoạn thận trọng trong nửa đầu 
năm. Về cuối năm, hoạt động xây dựng dự kiến tăng tốc khi điều kiện thi công 
thuận lợi hơn sau mùa mưa. Bên cạnh đó, việc triển khai các dự án nhà ở tại 
TP.HCM và các tỉnh lân cận được kỳ vọng hỗ trợ nhu cầu ống nhựa trong giai 
đoạn tới. Theo đó, chúng tôi dự phóng sản lượng tiêu thụ của BMP lần lượt đạt 
96,695/100,125/96,750 tấn trong 2026/2027/2028.

Biểu đồ 3. Tương quan giữa tỷ lệ chi phí bán hàng/doanh 
thu và lợi nhuận thuần/sản lượng tiêu thụ

Chí Minh

mức 5.3 lần, giảm 0.21 điểm QoQ do nhu 
cầu tiêu thụ toàn ngành suy giảm.

### Page 5

5

Lợi thế từ hệ sinh thái công ty mẹ 
SCG và đối tác Nhật Bản giúp BMP 
có nhiều dư địa để nâng cao hiệu 
quả hoạt động

Theo chia sẻ từ ban lãnh đạo, BMP có lợi thế vì nhập nguyên liệu từ công ty mẹ 
là hệ sinh thái SCG tại Việt Nam và đối tác Nhật Bản là AGC Chemicals Vietnam, 
giúp giảm được 5% thuế nhập khẩu và chi phí vận chuyển. Điều này giúp BMP 
hưởng lợi kép khi có thể tăng được giá bán theo thị trường nhưng nguyên vật 
liệu rẻ hơn so với các doanh nghiệp trong ngành. KBSV nhận thấy định hướng 
dài hạn của BMP là cân bằng lợi ích cổ đông và khách hàng, vì vậy việc giảm giá 
bán để giành thị phần không phải lựa chọn ưu tiên của doanh nghiệp mà là 
nâng cao tỷ suất lợi nhuận/sản phẩm thành phẩm.

KBSV dự báo biên lợi nhuận gộp thu hẹp trong 2H2026 khi chi phí nguyên liệu 
giá cao từ 1H2026 được phản ánh vào giá vốn, và hồi phục trong 2027–2028 
nhờ đặc tính giữ giá bán ít thay đổi của nhóm nhựa. Chúng tôi kỳ vọng giá PVC 
resin chịu áp lực giảm từ (1) nhu cầu xây dựng tại Trung Quốc còn yếu khi thị 
trường bất động sản chưa hồi phục rõ ràng; và (2) các nhà sản xuất giải phóng 
hàng tồn kho sau mùa mưa, làm tăng sản lượng hạt nhựa PVC resin. Theo đó, 
KBSV dự phóng biên lợi nhuận gộp của BMP lên 46.4%/46.7%/46.5% trong 
2026/2027/2028.

## Document 273: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

CAPITAL FLOWS BANKING
*** Japan is the main exception; the GPIF’s dollar
share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

## Document 283: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

to a 3-year high

## Document 300: French business confidence defies the turmoil for now

Source PDF: all report pdf/French business confidence defies the turmoil for now.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

October’s unexpected rebound in business conﬁdence is good
news, particularly for industry. But the survey does not yet
capture the impact of the latest social unrest, while higher
interest rates, a weak labour market and deteriorating public
ﬁnances are clouding the outlook further
Charlotte de Montpellier
An unexpected improvement
Against all expectations, business conﬁdence edged up in October
despite the sharp rise in French interest rates since September,

gaining one point to reach 97. It therefore remains below its long-
term average, but the rebound is a welcome surprise in the current
environment.
The main surprise came from industry, where the indicator jumped
four points to 105. The increase was largely concentrated in
transport equipment and mainly reﬂected an improvement in order
books, particularly foreign orders. The other components also
improved, but much more modestly. In services, the rebound was

### Page 2

limited to one point, taking the indicator to 99. This mainly
reﬂected stronger expectations for activity and demand, as well as
a marked recovery in administrative and support services. By
contrast, conﬁdence deteriorated in retail and construction.
On prices, the survey provides no new inﬂationary signal. Intentions
to raise prices declined in industry and remained stable in both
services and construction.
The rebound is encouraging, especially after several months of

disappointing industrial data. But it does not fundamentally alter
the outlook. The indicator of perceived economic uncertainty rose
sharply. In services, it even reached its highest level outside the
Covid-19 crisis.
More importantly, the employment climate fell three points to 95,
driven by a clear deterioration in hiring intentions in services
excluding temporary employment. The signal from the labour
market therefore remains ﬁrmly negative, which should limit

growth in household income and consumption over the coming
quarters.
Finally, the survey was conducted between late September and
early October. It therefore captures the widening in French spreads,
but not the violence and social unrest seen last weekend. October’s
rebound is encouraging, but fragile and already partly overtaken by
events.
Social unrest is already changing the picture
Since the October business conﬁdence data was collected, the
nature of the risk has changed. It no longer relates only to weak

activity and the impact of higher interest rates, but also to the
economic, political and ﬁnancial consequences of social unrest.
The economic cost of the violence is not limited to broken shop
windows and looting. As explained here, material damage
represents only the most visible part of the bill. Closures, lower
footfall and permanently lost sales could weigh far more heavily on
activity.

### Page 3

But the real issue is persistence. A brief and localised episode would
probably have a limited macroeconomic impact, while prolonged
unrest could undermine conﬁdence by encouraging households to
avoid city centres, tourists to postpone their trips and businesses to
delay investment. This could turn a temporary disruption into a
more lasting economic shock.
The unrest also makes it even more diﬃcult to secure
parliamentary approval for the budget, at a time when the

government has virtually no room to respond through additional
spending or tax cuts. France therefore has neither the ﬁscal room to
buy social peace nor the political room to impose the necessary
adjustment easily.
The vicious circle is under way
The reaction in ﬁnancial markets shows that the conﬁdence
channel is already at work. The yield on 10y French OATs has
crossed the symbolic 5% threshold, while the spread over the
German Bund has widened beyond 160bp, from 137bp last Friday.

These moves reﬂect both growing doubts about the ability of any
future government to pass the necessary reforms and savings
measures, and mounting concerns about the outlook for the French
economy.
This creates a vicious circle. At the same time, the renewed
deterioration in ﬁnancial conditions is further weakening an already
very poor growth outlook by increasing the cost of credit. We expect
GDP growth of just 0.3% in 2026 and 0.6% in 2027, but the risks are

tilted towards another downward revision. Against this backdrop,
the government’s objective of reducing the deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in
2027 looks particularly fragile, as it is based on growth of 0.5% in
2026 and 1% in 2027, well above our forecast. Even if the draft
budget were adopted in full, it might therefore not be enough, as
weaker activity would automatically reduce the expected tax
revenues.
A rating downgrade would merely conﬁrm
the obvious

On Friday, Moody’s is expected to formally acknowledge the
deterioration in France’s public ﬁnances. Our base case is a one-

notch downgrade from Aa3 to A1, with a stable outlook. This would
bring its rating into line with those of Fitch and S&P, which both rate
France A+ with a stable outlook. However, a negative outlook
following the downgrade cannot be ruled out. This would signal
that a further downgrade could follow in the relatively short term.
Fitch issued an unambiguous warning on 12 October. In the
absence of meaningful progress on the deﬁcit, the outlook could be

revised to negative, while another substantial increase in debt could
lead to a downgrade. Its base case now sees public debt reaching
almost 128% of GDP in 2030, with the deﬁcit still close to 5.5% and
the interest burden rising to 3.8% of GDP. An unscheduled rating
action before Fitch’s next review therefore cannot be ruled out if the
objective of reducing the deﬁcit to 5% in 2027 becomes clearly
unattainable.
These decisions do not change the diagnosis for France. They

merely provide formal conﬁrmation of a deterioration in the ﬁscal
position that is already widely visible. They are also a reminder
that, without a credible improvement in the growth and deﬁcit
outlook, rising debt and its associated interest burden will continue
to reduce France’s room for manoeuvre.

It looks like markets are going through a subtle regime shift.
Higher energy prices no longer mean higher rates forever;
instead, the long end is starting to express a little more fear
about growth. The upcoming US midterms may also be playing
a role. It looks like there is some defensive rotation underway
into the Swiss franc, the yen and sterling

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
As the US midterms approach, investors appear to be rotating into defensive
currencies
USD: Midterms could be starting to play
a role
Risk assets are on the back foot this European morning as energy
prices stay ﬁrm and tech stocks remain fragile. On the former, a
hefty risk premium remains in oil prices as attacks against Saudi
Arabia continue. On the latter, Q3 results from Meta and Tesla and
what they mean for capex plans are still being digested, but there
clearly remains a ﬁne line to walk. The suspicion now is that global
equity investors are starting to scale back their exposure to the tech

## Document 342: Persistent energy pressures have further to run (2026-10-22)

Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-22

Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than

elevated fuel and LNG prices.

durable US-Iran agreement accelerates

### Page 2

are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist
